市場焦點
美國
公債回購
• 美國財政部9月9日宣布，將於翌日回購最高60億美元的10至20年期美債，低於部分市場期待
的80億至100億美元規模 。民間持有美債規模達 31.8兆美元，相較之下 ，本次60億美元回購
僅約占民間持債規模 0.02%，對整體影響有限 。此外，約6成的美債期限不足 5年，意味未來
再融資需求仍高，若財政赤字未明顯收斂，長端殖利率上行壓力仍存。
日本央行
• 日本第二季GDP年化季增率上修至 1.4%(優於初值1.1%)，儘管伊朗戰爭引發原油供應緊張給
經濟帶來壓力，但GDP增速依舊高於0.7%潛在經濟成長率，雖然經濟表現支持日本央行(BOJ)
逐步實施貨幣政策正常化 ，但更關鍵的催化劑來自日美兩國協調干預匯市 ，遏制日圓極端貶
值的政治壓力，彭博經濟研究預期日本央行將在9月會議上加息25個基點。

美國
通膨
• 美國8月生產者物價指數 （PPI）年增5.4%，略高市場預期的 5.3%，核心PPI年增4.6%，符合
預期。8月消費者物價指數（CPI）年增3.35%、核心CPI年增2.4%，均符合市場預期，但核心
CPI月升幅0.3%，高於市場預期的 0.2%。數據公佈後，FedWatch顯示市場預期9月升息機率，
從前一周的50%攀升至86%。

美國8月通膨數據(CPI)
• 美國8月通膨數據：8月美國CPI月增0.4%、年增3.4%，核心CPI月增0.3%，核心服務通膨升溫至0.5%，顯示通膨降溫仍不
均衡，能源價格上漲為主要推手，市場預估8月核心PCE月增約0.3%、年增約3.8%，仍高於Fed 2%目標，搭配強勁就業數
據，9月升息1碼的壓力升高。不過，本次通膨主要來自能源、需求及AI投資帶動，與2021～2022年供給型通膨有所不同。
• 市場反應與展望：CPI公布後，2年期美債殖利率一度上升，惟長天期殖利率反而走低，顯示市場關注焦點並非單純升息，而
是Fed能否維持抗通膨政策可信度。整體而言，升息未必等同Risk-off，若經濟與企業獲利維持韌性，股市仍具支撐。
資料來源： investing.com，2026.9.14

能源成本回升推升PPI，通膨壓力牽動Fed政策空間
• 能源成本回升，PPI重新升溫：美國8月PPI月增0.4%、年增5.4%，增幅均明顯高於7月，主要受到商品價格上漲帶動；其
中最終需求商品價格月增1.1%，能源價格上升4.2%，柴油更是大漲逾2成，成為商品價格上升的重要來源，惟最終需求服
務價格僅略增0.1%，顯示本次PPI升溫仍以能源及商品價格為主。
• 成本壓力仍在，利率政策不確定性升高： 扣除食品、能源及貿易服務後的核心 PPI月增0.3%，微低於7月的0.4%，但年增
4.7%，顯示價格壓力仍未完全消退；其中運輸與倉儲服務價格上升2.3%，反映部分成本仍具黏著性，致使市場對Fed利率
政策的重新定價明顯升溫，進而推動股、債市短線波動擴大。
• 市場觀察：由於本次PPI升溫主要來自能源成本 ，但服務價格壓力整體仍相對溫和 ，因此尚不足以視為通膨全面升溫 ，然

能源價格若持續走高 ，將提高Fed維持較高利率的壓力 ，建議投資人短線宜降低長天期債券與高估值成長股的追價 ，債券
可優先配置短中天期、高品質債券，股票則可關注能源、金融及具定價能力企業，提高投組對利率與成本波動的承受度。
資料來源：U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics，Producer Price Indexes – Aug,  2026，2026.9.11
季調後PPI最終需求價格指數月變動
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.9.11

• 財政部對美債的回購規模不如預期：美國財政部9月9日宣布，將於翌日回購最高60億美元的10至20年期美債，主要鎖定流

動性較低的舊券，以改善長天期債券市場流動性。雖然本次回購高於先前設定的每次至少40億美元，但低於部分市場期待的
80億至100億美元規模。消息公布後10年期公債殖利率逼近5%，30年期殖利率亦突破5.3%。
• 回購規模在高額赤字背景下，難以有效拉低長天期公債殖利率：美國今年公債發行規模較2025年增加11.8%，民間持有美債
規模達31.8兆美元、年增8.2%，相較之下，本次60億美元回購僅約占民間持債規模0.02%，對整體影響有限。此外，約6成
的美債期限不足5年，意味未來再融資需求仍高，若財政赤字未明顯收斂，長端殖利率上行壓力仍存。反觀新興國家基本面
持續改善，且當地債市有貨幣潛在升值空間，可作為全球債券配置的分散來源。

地緣政治動盪推升油價，但全球經濟依然展現韌性
市場共識未來12 個月預期每股盈餘

全球主要市場及國家經濟成長率
新興市場
美股
日股
歐股
• 地緣政治動盪推升油價，但全球經濟依然展現韌性：美國和伊朗的衝突已經進入第7個月，能源價格上升及地緣政治風險仍
對全球經濟與市場信心形成壓力，但AI投資熱潮持續推升科技需求，使全球經濟展現優於預期的韌性。其中，亞洲仍是全球
成長動能最強的區域之一，2026年亞洲（不含日本）經濟成長率預估達4.7%，明顯高於全球平均2.9%及成熟市場1.6%。在
內需與科技出口支撐下，新興亞洲基本面相對穩健，具備較佳的中長期配置條件。

• AI資本支出延續，台韓成為核心受惠市場：AI資本支出持續擴張，2026年全球雲端AI資本支出預估達7,960億美元，並帶動
AI半導體市場至2030年突破7,500億美元。亞洲掌握全球半導體供應鏈核心，台灣受惠於先進製程與先進封裝需求，韓國則
受惠於HBM記憶體需求，台韓可望成為AI基礎建設擴張的主要受惠市場；同時，中國與印度則提供較穩定的內需及經濟成
長支撐。2026年亞洲企業盈餘成長率預估達72.13%，顯著高於全球平均，建議新興亞洲以台韓科技股為主要成長配置，並
搭配中國、印度市場分散區域風險。

顧
短債供給增加未引發融資緊張，短天期債券防禦價值提升
• 回購市場維持穩定，短債供給暫未造成壓力：美國回購市場今年以來維持平穩，儘管9月短債償付與企業繳稅可能帶來短
暫資金波動，但SOFR與IORB利差仍處相對正常區間 ，顯示目前準備金與短期流動性尚稱充裕 。此外SOFR/IORB差值對
短期美債淨發行增加的敏感度明顯低於2025年底，反映流動性環境偏寬鬆。
• 貨幣基金成長放緩，供給吸收能力受到限制：零售資金流入偏弱，使貨幣市場基金資產增速落後 ，2026年迄今僅增加約
325億美元，遠低於2025年同期的2,050億美元；即便Q4季節性回升，新增資金對短債供給的承接能力仍可能有限。
• 後市觀察：年底前短債淨發行規模預估約3,250億美元，後續需關注短債供給、企業繳稅及財政部長端公債回購對資金面

的影響。建議投資人配置上可提高 1～3年期美國國庫券或短天期美債 ETF比重，以取得票息收益並降低長端殖利率波動
對債券價格的影響。
資料來源：US Treasury，FRBNY ，Federal Reserve ，2026.9.9
美債淨供應 vs. SOFR/IORB差值

風險偏好升溫與債券供給增加，推升期限溢價與長債殖利率
• 全球期限溢酬上升，長債面臨重新定價：全球債券殖利率同步走高，不僅反映各國政策利率預期，也受到全球期限溢酬上升
推動；投資人對持有長天期政府債券要求更高補償 ，顯示長債殖利率上行已具有跨市場的結構性因素 。Bloomberg
Economics的模型亦顯示，全球期限溢酬是近年G7長債殖利率走高的重要共同因素。
• 風險資產吸引資金，放大長債供需壓力：AI投資題材推升股票市場、信用利差仍相對偏窄，加上大宗商品走強，使股票、公
司債及商品與政府債券形成資金競爭；在政府融資需求持續增加的情況下 ，若債券需求未同步擴張，政府公債需提供更高殖
利率才能吸引資金。
• 後市觀察：長端利率已成為股債配置的重要變數 ，若殖利率因財政供給與風險偏好維持高檔 ，長天期債券及高估值股票均可

能承壓；建議以短中天期美債及高品質投資級公司債為核心 ，待長端殖利率充分反映期限溢酬後，再分批拉長債券存續期間。
資料來源：Bloomberg Economics，2026.9.9

日本央行升息箭在弦上，但缺乏連續升息條件
• GDP修正支持升息 ，但關鍵點在配合日本和美國政府遏制日圓疲軟： 日本第二季 GDP年化季增率上修至 1.4%(優於初值
1.1%)，儘管伊朗戰爭引發原油供應緊張給經濟帶來壓力，但GDP增速依舊高於0.7%潛在經濟成長率，雖然經濟表現支持日
本央行(BOJ)逐步實施貨幣政策正常化 ，但更關鍵的催化劑來自日美兩國協調干預匯市 ，遏制日圓極端貶值的政治壓力 ，彭
博經濟研究預期日本央行將在9月會議上加息25個基點。
• 缺乏連續大幅加息的支撐力道： (1)經濟衝擊風險：日圓升值效應已對經濟產生緊縮效果 ，彭博預估可能拖累實際 GDP成長
達0.3%。(2)財政負擔過重：若政策利率升至 1.75%以上，將大幅增加政府利息支出 ，擠壓高市內閣的財政刺激空間 。(3)企

業投資放緩：私人資本支出仍顯疲軟(季比下降0.9%)，顯示經濟復甦基礎尚未完全穩固。
• 小結：日本央行在政治、匯率與經濟基本面交集下 ，9月升息已成定局，但考量財政負擔與全球金融穩定後 ，其後續加息步
伐應偏向謹慎與循序漸進 ，密切關注9月日本央行會議後聲明 ，若央行強調循序漸進而非連續緊縮 ，市場波動相對可控；反
之，若釋放過度鷹派訊號，則需防範市場拋售風險。由於市場資金對日圓套利交易依賴度仍高，日圓任何短期內的急速升值，
都極易觸發全球風險資產的連鎖去槓桿效應。

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.8

中國8月通膨回升，惟需求端疲弱
• 8月份 CPI及PPI：消費者物 價指 數 (CPI)月增 0.4%(前月月減 0.1%)、年增 0.8%(前月 0.5%)，生 產者物價指數 (PPI) 月增
0.4%(前月月減0.7%)、年增3.8%(前月3.5%)。通膨走揚主要受到能源及通訊設備成本上升影響。
• 觀點：中國自2023年以來，內需市場面臨房地產崩盤 、青年失業率飆升以及公共債務攀升等困境 。今年年初以來，通膨雖
然由負轉正，不過從細項得知，因國際油價飆升、AI硬體設備面臨漲價壓力，本波通膨回升主要是受到供給端影響。外部成
本壓力恐進一步壓抑中國內需消費，中國要在真正意義上走出通縮環境可能還需要更多官方政策發力。彭博預估，在需求未
出現明顯改善的情境下，中國央行需要近一步放寬貨幣政策，預測年底前可能降息10個基點。

資料來源：Bloomberg，截至2026.9.9。

AI產業獲利亮眼，但需留意變現能力和資本效率
• AI獲利增速領先大盤，銷售成長成為獲利關鍵：AI板塊在2026年第一與第二季分別實現52.8%與59.7%的高獲利成長，受高
基期影響，未來四季度平均獲利成長預計放緩至 34.4%，但仍大幅優於非 AI板塊12.3%。預計2026年第三季至2027年第二
季，AI平均銷售成長率將達 27.2%，為非AI成分股(5%)5倍以上 。隨著未來利潤率擴張放緩 (預計未來 6個季度平均僅提升
1.66%，低於過去12個季度的3.52%)，持續的銷售成長是維持獲利的關鍵。
• AI供應鏈內部發展並不均衡： 半導體最為強勁，2026Q3至2027Q2預期銷售成長65.8%，獲利大增112.1%且淨利潤率大幅
擴張8.86%，為產業鏈核心引擎。雲端巨頭表現穩健，營收與獲利增速維持約26%，基礎設施軟體與硬體也有15%~20%營

收與獲利成長 。但應用端軟體成長相對乏力 ，未來四季度獲利預期增速僅 4.3%與6.5%，表現低於標普500非AI股平均水準。
• 資本強度創新高 ，但自由現金流承壓： AI資本支出佔營收比重自 2024年Q1的8.9%一路攀升至 2026年Q2的20.1%，預計
2026年下半年將達到23%的高點。資本支出激增導致自由現金流與資本支出之比(FCF/Capex)從2.48倍滑落至0.69倍，自由
現金流利潤率亦從21%降至13.9%。隨著現金逐漸消耗，企業可能需透過債券市場進行發債融資 ，以維持龐大基礎設施投資。
• 小結與投資建議：AI板塊依然是美股獲利驅動力，但隨利潤率提升空間受限、自由現金流受資本支出壓制，投資邏輯已從全
面暴漲轉向注重變現能力與資本效率。投資建議聚焦上游硬體與半導體，建議持續優選具備高定價能力與實質訂單支撐的半
導體與雲端基礎設施龍頭，留意資本支出佔比過高且自由現金流急劇惡化公司，優先選擇現金流充沛、融資成本低企業。
標普500指數盈利成長：AI vs. 非AI
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.7

標普500指數剔除AI後利潤率或下降

歐央9月升息一碼，措辭更鷹經濟預測上修，12月或再升息
• 歐洲央行9月升息一碼：歐央將三大政策利率各上調25個基點，存款利率升至
2.5%，反映能源價格受地緣政治因素推升，同時上調2027年和2028年的核心
通膨預測分別上修了 0.1個百分點。與此同時，對於經濟成長前景更為樂觀 ，
上調了今年的GDP成長預測，主要反映了2026年第二季數據優於預期以及下
半年內需表現更為穩健 ，此外，年末成長動能的增強為 2027年創造了更有利
的延續效應，也推動了明年經濟成長預期的上調。
• 投資建議： 此次會後 聲明基調偏鷹 ，強調通膨衝擊預計還將延續 ，市場預期
12月或再升息 ，這將有助銀行業利差 ，進一步支撐營收與獲利表現 ，同時，
企業投資與融資需求回升，加上製造業景氣改善帶動放款成長，均有助於歐洲

銀行業擴大收益來源， 建議投資人可以適度配置，增加投組多元性。

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.14
• 美國8月通膨數據：8月美國CPI月增0.4%、年增3.4%，核心CPI月增0.3%，核心服務通膨升溫至0.5%，顯示通膨降溫仍不均衡，
能源價格上漲為主要推手，市場預估8月核心PCE月增約0.3%、年增約3.8%，仍高於Fed 2%目標，搭配強勁就業數據，9月升息
1碼的壓力升高。不過，本次通膨主要來自能源、需求及AI投資帶動，與2021～2022年供給型通膨有所不同。
• 市場反應與展望：CPI公布後，2年期美債殖利率一度上升，惟長天期殖利率反而走低，顯示市場關注焦點並非單純升息，而是

Fed能否維持抗通膨政策可信度。整體而言，升息未必等同Risk-off，若經濟與企業獲利維持韌性，股市仍具支撐。

2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

### Page 1

证券研究报告

## Document 102: 261014_daiwa_ ITEQ

Source PDF: all report pdf/261014_daiwa_ ITEQ.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We preview ITEQ’s 3Q26 results (due on 6 November). In our 
previous note, “Upgrading: strong beat in 2Q26 with improving outlook” (7 
August), we had underestimated the magnitude and durability of price hikes 
for E-glass and M4-and-below CCL. We raise our 2026-28E earnings by 
43-70% to reflect better-than-expected pricing and gross margin 
performance, which leads to our higher TP of TWD1,110 (from TWD600).

What’s the impact: Ongoing price hike of E-glass and M4-and-below 
CCL. This week, a major Japanese copper-clad laminate (CCL) vendor 
raised its entry-level CCL price by 200% to catch up with other global 
peers’ price hike level. One of the Chinese peers is also having a constant 
c.10% ASP hike on a monthly basis in 2026. We are increasingly confident 
in the management’s target for its blended price hike in 2H26. We now 
forecast c.30%/20% price hikes in 3Q26E and 4Q26E, respectively (vs. our 
previous estimates of 20%/10%). Looking ahead, we expect another 10% 
price hike per quarter in 1H27 (c.20% price hike in total), while pricing 
should gradually stabilise in 2H27 given the rising supply of E-glass, in our 
view. While the visibility towards 2H27 and 2028 remains relatively limited, 
we believe the key upside risks to our current forecast will be the stronger-
than-expected price hike magnitude and durability of the current up-cycle.

3Q26 results preview. After factoring in stronger-than-expected price 
hikes, ITEQ’s 3Q26 revenue grew c.35% QoQ, mainly driven by the 30% 
price hike despite utilisation rate staying at c.75%. We lift our gross margin 
forecast to c.30.3% (from 24.7% in our August note), in line with the August 
preliminary results’ pre-tax margin of 25.8%. With price hikes continuing, 
we expect the 4Q26E gross margin to expand to c.35%. Given disciplined 
capacity expansion through 2028, we expect the higher GM to be sustained 
through 2028. Beyond price hike, we believe the key longer-term growth 
drivers include: a) rising regular server demand and new platform adoption 
(using M7 grade CCL) in 2027; and b) M9 certification by more clients.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 43-70% to factor in 
the price hikes. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating, and raise our 12M TP to 
TWD1,110 (from TWD600), based on an unchanged target PER of 22x 
(higher than the range during the past 12 months of 5-18x) applied to our 1-
year-forward EPS. While we expect the current upcycle will eventually ease 
starting from mid-2027, we remain positive on the near-term price outlook. 
Key downside risk: weaker-than-expected regular server demand.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 36-60% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, mainly due to our more aggressive view on ITEQ’s price hikes 
and margin profile.

14 October 2026

3Q26 results preview: better-than-expected price hikes

➢ Expecting better price hikes from 2H26 to 1H27 
➢ Spec upgrades driven by new CPU platform adoption in 2027 
➢ Reiterating our Buy (1) rating; raising our 12M TP to TWD1,110

Company profile 
ITEQ Corporation is a leading manufacturer of high performance copper clad laminate materials used 
for the fabrication of printed circuit boards and was founded in April of 1997. ITEQ’s products find use in 
many different application areas, such as computational and communications applications, Radio 
Frequency and Microwave devices, Automotive applications, and High Density Interconnect solutions.

### Page 4

BT substrate supply/demand gap at 11%/14% in 2027/28E on the back of solid memory demand

We introduce our 2026-28E BT substrate TAM forecast, expanding at a 32% 2026-28E 
CAGR to reach US$16.8bn in 2028E (up from US$7.4bn in 2025; Exhibit 1). This growth is 
primarily driven by solid memory demand in general servers (general servers will account 
for 32% of total BT TAM in 2028E, up from 21% in 2025; Exhibit 2), with increasing 
memory capacity/BT content per server. We expect the memory BT substrate TAM to 
outpace the broader BT substrate TAM with a 41% 2026-28E CAGR, accounting for 77% 
of total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (was 56% in 2025). 
On the other hand, we expect the capacity growth rate to decelerate vs. the previous 
upcycle in 2020-22 (6% 2026-28E CAGR vs. 9% 2020-22 CAGR), as key Asian substrate 
suppliers are placing greater focus on their ABF substrate business, and are hence 
scaling back their BT substrate capacity (including Unimicron, which held 8% market 
share in 2025). 
As such, we now expect the overall BT substrate supply/demand gap to reach 
1%/11%/14% in 2H26/2027/2028 (Exhibit 3), while major BT suppliers have started to 
reduce capacity from 2026, potentially leading to a supply shortage in 2H26. We expect 
BT capacity utilization rates (UTR) to trend up amid a supply-constrained environment 
(surpassing 100% from 2027E per GSe), providing a better BT pricing environment going 
forward. The BT substrate supply tightness since the start of the year has directly led to 
a 10% YTD increase in overall BT substrate pricing, and we believe pricing momentum 
for BT substrates will remain robust in 2H26-2028E. 
Accordingly, we forecast overall BT substrate prices will further increase by 
15%-20%/10%-15% in 2027/28E, benefiting BT substrates’ profitability/pricing for 
Taiwanese players (GMs ranged from 15%-27% in 2Q26 and are projected to increase to 
18%-34%/19%-36%+ in 2027/28E; Exhibit 4).

AI; and (2) upcoming launches of new general server CPU platforms, which will drive

(down from 51% in 2025; Exhibit 7). We expect general server BT substrate TAM to grow 
at a faster 42% 2026-28E CAGR, accounting for 32% of total BT substrate TAM in 2028E 
(up from 21% in 2025). Meanwhile, we expect AI server BT substrate TAM to see the 
faster growth among all applications at a 73% 2026-28E CAGR, reaching 9% of total BT 
substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 2% in 2025).

Solid growth in general server BT substrate TAM will support mid- to long-term BT 
market growth 
We expect general server BT substrate TAM to deliver a 42% 2026-28E CAGR and reach 
US$5.3bn in 2028E (up from US$1.6bn in 2025; Exhibit 8), which will account for 32% of 
total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 21% in 2025). We expect general server 
memory BT substrate TAM to grow slightly faster at a 43% 2026-28E CAGR and reach 
US$5.0bn in 2028E (up from US$1.4bn in 2025), accounting for 39% of total memory BT 
substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 20% in 2025). This is driven by solid general server 
shipments (we expect general server shipments to register an 8% 2026-28E CAGR; see 
here; Exhibit 9) alongside increasing memory capacity/BT content per general server 
(Exhibit 10). 
Our US analyst, James Schneider, expects Intel/AMD general server CPU shipment to 
grow at a 28%/36% 2026-28E CAGR, reaching a combined 59mn units in 2028E (vs. 
26mn units in 2025). This supports our constructive view that general servers are the key 
driver of the current BT substrate upcycle, given the strong correlation between memory 
BT substrate TAM growth and general server CPU shipment growth.

The strong growth in memory BT substrate TAM is largely driven by memory shipments

### Page 8

Strong AI server shipment growth in 2026-28E will also support BT TAM expansion 
We expect AI server BT substrate TAM to deliver a 73% 2026-28E CAGR and reach 
US$1,476mn in 2028E (up from US$160mn in 2025; Exhibit 14), accounting for 9% of 
total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 2% in 2025). We expect AI server memory BT 
substrate TAM to grow slightly faster at a 75% 2026-28E CAGR and reach US$1,428mn 
in 2028E (up from US$145mn in 2025), accounting for 9% of total memory BT substrate 
TAM in 2028E (up from 2% in 2025). This is driven by solid growth in AI server shipments, 
as we expect AI servers (8-GPU equivalent) and AI server racks (72-GPU equivalent) to 
register 17%/71% 2026-28E CAGRs (see here) with increasing memory capacity per 
server/per server rack. 
We expect GPU/CPU attach rates to move from 2x to 1.1-1.4x over time as agentic AI 
applications become mainstream, implying that each GPU deployment will require more 
server CPUs (see our US analyst Jim Schneider’s Intel initiation). Given that memory BT 
content in AI servers is primarily tied to server CPU count, if the GPU/CPU attach rate 
narrows from 2.0x to 1.1-1.4x, we estimate 1%/1%-2% upside to memory BT substrate 
TAM in 2027/28E. 
For AI server racks (72-GPU equivalent), BT content per rack continues to expand mainly 
due to increasing demand for more memory capacity, resulting in a 50% 2026-28E 
CAGR in BT content per AI server rack (Exhibit 15). A key upgrade is that Nvidia’s VR200 
will support higher DRAM capacity, implying a 100% increase in DRAM memory BT 
substrate content compared to GB200/GB300. We also note Nvidia is releasing a 
standalone Vera CPU rack designed for agentic AI, implying further demand upside for 
AI CPU shipments and memory BT substrates, as each Vera CPU rack incorporates 8x 
more DRAM memory BT substrate content compared to VR200.

### Page 9

Smartphones remain critical to BT demand with growth going forward tracking 
slower than the overall BT industry 
We expect overall smartphone BT substrate TAM to grow at a 24% 2026-28E CAGR 
(tracking slower than the overall BT industry CAGR of 32%) to reach US$6.5bn in 2028E 
(up from US$3.8bn in 2025), accounting for 39% of total 2028E BT TAM (down from 
51% in 2025). We expect smartphone memory BT substrate TAM to grow at a faster 36% 
2026-28E CAGR to reach US$2.4bn in 2028E (up from US$1.0bn in 2025), accounting 
for 19% of total memory BT TAM in 2028E (down from 24% in 2025). 
As mentioned in our previous update on the BT substrate industry (see here), we believe 
the previous upcycle (in 2020-22) was mainly driven by the proliferation of SiP/AiP 
designs that increased BT substrate content per device, especially for smartphones. 
While smartphones remain critical to overall BT demand, accounting for 51% of total 
2025 BT substrate TAM, we expect their contribution to moderate going forward 
(reaching 39% of 2028E BT TAM). This is driven by softer smartphone shipment volumes 
(we now forecast smartphone shipments at -10%/+2%/+2% YoY in 2026-28E; see here) 
and the fact that BT substrate content per smartphone will be mainly driven by higher 
memory BT substrate pricing (Exhibit 16) rather than other meaningful content value 
gains (memory will account for 46% of smartphone BT ASP in 2028E, up from 32% in 
2025). 
We expect 25%/26%/28% of overall smartphone shipments to be equipped with 
mmWave connectivity in 2026/27/28E (Exhibit 17), resulting in an 18% 2026-28E CAGR 
for smartphone AiP BT TAM to reach US$1,128mn in 2028 (up from US$713mn in 2025). 
For iPhones, mmWave connectivity is only available for models sold in the US 
(representing 35%-40% of total iPhone shipments per GSe). In the Android camp, 
Samsung’s flagship smartphones that support mmWave connectivity are also only for 
the US market.

### Page 10

Automotive BT consumption to steadily increase in coming years 
We expect automotive BT TAM to grow at a 34% 2026-28E CAGR and reach US$777mn 
in 2028E (up from US$317mn in 2025), which will account for 5% of total 2028E BT TAM 
(up from 4% in 2025). This stronger-than-industry growth is mainly driven by rising EV 
penetration going forward (EV penetration rate will increase from 14% in 2025 to 20% in 
2028E per our Japanese auto team; Exhibit 19) and the corresponding increase in 
electronics content per car, which requires more BT substrates. Furthermore, we believe 
rising autonomous driving penetration will result in an increase in electronic 
components/BT consumption per car.

BT market revenue to grow at a 28% 2026-28E CAGR with sustainable memory 
demand 
We project BT substrate market revenue to grow at a 28% CAGR in 2026-28E and reach 
US$14.6bn in 2028E (up from US$6.9bn in 2025). Despite this solid growth, total BT 
market revenue will account for less than 1% of customers’ overall revenue in 2026-28E 
(down from 2%-3% in the previous upcycle in 2020-22), as customers’ overall growth is 
tracking faster at a 34% 2026-28E CAGR. We believe this lower ratio of BT market

BT market revenue as a percentage of total customers’ revenue peaked during the 
2020-22 upcycle (at 2.5%-2.6%), when demand was primarily driven by smartphones

substrates for GPUs/CPUs/ASICs, while BT substrate spending is more focused on 
lower-end BT substrates for memory applications.

BT supply analysis: Major suppliers starting to reduce capacity, leading to slower capacity growth vs. 
previous upcycle

We believe overall BT industry supply growth in this upcycle will remain modest, as key 
Asian BT substrate suppliers are decreasing capacity, which will partially offset capacity 
additions from smaller players. Thus, we expect supply to grow at a 6% 2026-28E 
CAGR, compared to the 9% CAGR during the 2020-22 upcycle. This is mainly due to 
Unimicron transferring BT substrate capacity to high-end large-body-size ABF 
substrates (see here), while SEMCO is focusing more on expanding its ABF substrate 
capacity (see here) with its BT substrate business mainly focusing on memory and

We expect BT capacity for Taiwanese suppliers to contract at a -1% 2026-28E CAGR, 
mainly due to Unimicron’s capacity reductions. We expect BT capacity for Korean 
suppliers to grow at a 3% 2026-28E CAGR, while mainland Chinese suppliers expand at 
an 11% 2026-28E CAGR. Overall, we expect Taiwanese/Korean/mainland Chinese BT 
suppliers to account for 27%/41%/16% of total revenue share in 2028E respectively 
(was 23%/45%/12% in 2025). 
The BT substrate supply market remains fragmented, with the top 5 suppliers accounting 
for 53% of total industry revenue in 2025. Over the next few years, we expect a slight 
change in the competitive landscape as major suppliers reduce capacity against a 
backdrop of robust demand expanding at a 32% 2026-28E CAGR, as outlined above.

We believe market players will increase BT substrate capacity by only 4%/3%/9% in 
2026/27/28E. Taiwanese players, such as NYPCB and Kinsus, mentioned they will 
primarily seek de-bottlenecking instead of investing in new equipment. However, ZDT 
mentioned it will expand BT capacity at its Qinhuangdao plants 1 & 2 starting in 2H26, 
and the company expects this expansion to increase its existing BT substrate capacity by 
20%. 
As for major mainland Chinese players, Shennan (002916.SZ; Buy; covered by Ting Song) 
will continue to expand its IC substrate business; the company expects Phase 2 of its 
Guangzhou factory to start production in 4Q26 (see here). Fastprint (002436.SZ; Not 
Covered), which will hold 3% of total market share in 2026 based on BBG consensus, has 
announced in June an investment of Rmb1.2bn for further BT capacity expansion; this 
translates to an additional 25k square meter monthly capacity in the coming years (vs. 
its current 50k square meter monthly capacity). 
Furthermore, we see Korean suppliers taking a more proactive stance toward future BT 
capacity additions. In June, LG Innotek (011070.KS; Neutral; covered by Guini Lee) 
announced plans to invest US$1bn to further expand its substrate capacity in Vietnam, 
mainly for RF-SiP , BT and ABF substrates. For SimmTech (222800.KQ; Not Covered), the 
company plans to invest KRW105bn to expand BT substrate capacity specifically for

### Page 14

We have seen BT substrate demand improve since the start of 2026 alongside a firmer 
pricing outlook, and we expect demand growth to consistently outpace supply in 
2026-28E (demand CAGR of 15% vs. supply CAGR of 6%). We believe industry utilization 
rates (UTR) will continue to trend up over the coming years (reaching 100%+ after 
2027E) due to slow supply growth going forward. 
Memory predominantly requires wire bonding chip scale package (WBCSP) technology, 
and this production capacity is not interchangeable with flip-chip chip-scale package 
(FCCSP) technology (where smartphone APs represent the biggest demand driver). As 
such, we believe memory BT substrate supply will remain tight through at least 1H28, as 
new dedicated memory capacity will only come online in 2028. Overall BT pricing has 
already risen by 10% YTD, partially driven by higher material costs (including substrate 
CCL, copper foil, gold, etc., which account for 60% of total BT substrate BOM; Exhibit 
26). Going forward, we believe the slow increase in BT substrate supply, especially 
WBCSP capacity, will be the key price hike driver rather than simply reflecting the 
material cost increase. Furthermore, BT substrates typically account for less than 5% of 
total memory BOM, providing ample room for further BT price hikes amid the rising 
memory pricing trend.

### Page 15

With favorable BT substrate supply/demand conditions, we believe the profitability of 
key BT vendors will see a structural increase in the coming years. Similar to current 
dynamics in the ABF market, the BT substrate industry’s profitability profile has 
navigated three upcycles and three downcycles over the past 20 years (Exhibit 27). 
Overall margin profiles for key BT vendors have begun improving since the start of 2026, 
supported by increasing memory demand amid a supply-constrained environment, 
especially following several years of slow capex investment. We believe this profitability 
enhancement will continue in 2H26-2028, supported by sustained industry demand 
uptrend.

### Page 16

Optical module mSAP is seeing a widening S/D gap, prompting a shift of BT capacity to meet strong 
demand

We introduce our 2026-28E 800G+ optical module mSAP TAM forecast, expanding at an 
87% 2026-28E CAGR to reach US$7.1bn by 2028E (up from US$0.5bn in 2025; Exhibit 
28). This is based on our 800G+ optical module shipment estimates of a 48% 2026-28E 
CAGR (see here). The connectivity PCB within the 800G+ optical module requires mSAP 
technology, where several PCB makers have exposure to and are able to leverage their 
extensive mSAP experience from smartphone substrate-like PCB (SLP) production. 
On the other hand, major PCB and substrate suppliers are placing greater focus on this 
emerging business and pursuing proactive capacity expansion plans, especially ZDT and 
Compeq (the latter aims to ramp optical module mSAP capacity to reach monthly 
revenue of ~NT$3bn by end 2027 vs. less than NT$100mn as of 2Q26; see here). 
Although Unimicron was a front-runner in the 800G+ optical module mSAP business in 
2025 (29% market share in 2025 per GSe), the company is placing greater focus on its 
ABF substrate business, and hence leaving room for emerging peers to capture market 
share through aggressive expansion plans and technology leadership. 
We believe ZDT will become the market share leader in the optical module mSAP 
industry, as we expect the company to expand mSAP capacity by 5x over the next two 
years, positioning it well to gain share in a supply-constrained environment (our industry 
checks indicate mSAP is the most supply-constrained component in the 1.6T optical 
transceiver supply chain). Furthermore, the company currently holds an industry-leading 
yield of 90%+ for its optical module mSAP products. 
Overall, we expect the 800G+ optical module mSAP supply/demand gap to reach 
5%/25%/38% in 2026/27/28E (Exhibit 29). Although major mSAP suppliers continue 
to have proactive expansion plans, this appears insufficient to keep pace with strong 
demand. The 800G+ optical module mSAP tightness since the start of 2026 has directly 
led to a 20%-30% pricing increase YTD, and we believe the pricing outlook for 800G+ 
optical module mSAP will remain solid in 2027/28E.

### Page 23

Investment thesis, PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - Zhen Ding Technology 
Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT) is the global leading PCB supplier (top 1 in market share in 
sales) with 7-10% market share, and started its ABF substrate business from 2023 in 
Shenzhen, China; it has the highest ABF revenue exposure to the China market compared 
to all other ABF substrate suppliers. We believe ZDT will hold more than 40% ABF 
substrate market share in China by 2027E (up from 31% in 2024) and benefit the most 
from the strong and growing China ABF substrate market (53% 2025-27E CAGR) due to 
the increasing in-sourcing ratio for the AI computing ASIC/GPU market in China. We also 
expect the increasing foldable smartphone penetration rate in the long term to drive 
ZDT’s FPC TAM, and account for 39% of the company’s total revenue in 2027E. We see 
further revenue upside potential from foldable phones. We are Buy rated mainly due to 
our positive view on the company’s China ABF substrate business opportunity, which 
should not only drive ZDT’s revenue growth, but also improve its OPM level in the long 
term. We see upside for the stock price considering the company’s solid growth 
opportunity, and our TP implied P/E multiple is still lower than the company’s past 10+ 
year P/E average, which we believe is fair considering the potential upside from China 
ABF substrate market and increasing foldable phone penetration rate.  
Valuation: Our 12-month TP of NT$1,025 is based on a SOTP valuation methodology, 
including (1) 3.5x blended 2028E P/B for ABF & BT substrate, (2) 20x 2028E P/E for 
high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in line with industry average), and (3) 8x 
2028E P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB (in line with industry downcycle 
average valuation). 
Key downside risks include: (1) weaker-than-expected iPhone demand or slower 
content upgrade, (2) delay in new substrate capacity or slower-than-expected yield rate 
ramp up progress, and (3) fiercer competition in China ABF market.

### Page 24

Investment Thesis - Kinsus
Kinsus is one of the key IC substrates suppliers globally with ~10% ABF/BT substrates
market share in 2024, with ~18% of total 2025 revenue contributed by its contact lens
subsidiary, Pegavision (6941.TW, Not Covered). With our view that the ABF substrate
shortage started from 2Q26 (ABF substrate return to supply demand balance from late
2025), we believe Kinsus’ performance will continue to improve compared with high-end

ABF peers considering the company’s increasing market share in the high-end ABF
substrate market; however, ~36% of the company’s revenue was contributed by BT
substrates in 2025 and 30% of the company’s ABF revenue is from non-LTA customers,
which we believe will continue to enjoy a better pricing outlook. As a result, we believe
Kinsus’ earnings outlook should continue to be strong (~118% 2026-29E CAGR), mainly
driven by better BT substrate and non-LTA ABF substrate pricing outlook, despite the

unfavorable demand outlook before 1H26. We believe Kinsus is an experienced
substrate supplier with a clear capacity expansion plan that can also upgrade its product
portfolio in the long term. We believe we are at the beginning of an industry upcycle,
making us positive on Kinsus’ stock performance over the next 12 months.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Kinsus
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,515 for Kinsus is based on

19.5x 2028E P/E, which is +2.0x STDV higher than Kinsus’ past upcycle average P/E
(14.2x).
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)
Slower-than-expected ABF substrates pricing upgrading outlook, (3)
Slower/Less-than-expected AI server orders from 2H26.
Investment Thesis - NYPCB
NYPCB manufactures and markets printed circuit boards (PCBs) and integrated circuit
(IC) substrates (including ABF and BT substrates). We are Buy rated on NYPCB

considering the company’s high exposure to BT substrates and non-LTA ABF substrate
(70%+ of total revenue), where we expect GM/OPM to increase significantly in the
coming quarters (from breakeven in OPM in 1H25 to 26/42% in 2026/27E) due to the
favorable substrate pricing outlook. Despite the company’s low exposure to high-end
ABF substrate industry (<20% of total revenue in 2025), we believe the expanding
demand from ASIC AI server and high-end switch IC demand will drive the company’s

high-end ABF revenue exposure to 40%+ by 2027E, suggesting better GM/OPM outlook
in the long term. In 2H25-2H26, we believe NYPCB will continue to benefit from stronger
ABF/BT pricing and should be a key beneficiary of the pricing uptrend considering the
company’s key focus on BT and non-LTA ABF substrate industries. Moreover, in the long
term (after 2026), we expect the ABF industry to return to supply tightness, and NYPCB’s
expanding high-end exposure will lead to a long-term earnings growth outlook (148%

2026-28E earnings CAGR). As a result, we see solid upside potential for NYPCB’s
earnings and stock performance.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - NYPCB
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$2,700 for NYPCB is based
on 21x 2028E P/E, which is +1.5x STDV higher than the past upcycle average P/E (13.3x).
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)

Slower-than-expected ABF substrates pricing upgrading outlook, and (3)

Investment Thesis - Unimicron Technology
Unimicron is a key supplier of ABF substrates (25% market share in 2025) and BT
substrates and also produces FPC/HDI/PCB products. We are Neutral rated on the
shares given long-term growth opportunity prospects: (1) over 70% of ABF shipments
are covered by long-term agreements (LTA), which limits pricing flexibility and makes

price hike less likely; (2) the company’s execution track record has been weaker than
peers; (3) capacity expansion plan remains slower than peers, constraining its ability to
capture incremental AI demand. The current ABF substrates market is seeing more
favorable supply demand outlook, and Unimicron is likely to see better pricing outlook
to reflect rising material costs in the coming quarters. AI ABF account for 10% of the
company’s total revenue, but the growth is likely to be slower than peers due to the

company continuing to lose market share to peers given its less favourable production
yield and slow capacity expansion. Overall, we are Neutral rated on Unimicron given our
outlook for limited upside potential, barring ASIC AI servers, despite our generally
positive view in the long term on its profitability trajectory.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Unimicron Technology
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,270 for Unimicron is

based on 17.4x 2028E P/E, which is 2.7x STDV higher than the past upcycle average P/E
(10.3x).
Key upside/downside risks: (1) Slower/faster-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)
Slower/faster-than-expected ABF substrates upgrading pace, and (3) Slower/faster
than-expected AI server PCB market share loss progress.

Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

(reclassified from COGS).
Other takeaways:

• AI packaging remains an execution watchpoint – 3D AI foundry's revenue
decreased from 5.4% of the mix in 2Q to 1.4% in 3Q during the relocation,
although interposers are expected to be fully loaded by year-end.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Powerchip Semiconductor Manufacturing Co (6770.TW)
Base case, 2.0x 2027e P/B. This method is in line with our approach for Greater China memory
IDM players, in view of the industry's high volatility. Our 2.0x target P/B is higher than the
historical average of 1.5x since 2021, reflecting our optimistic view on improved pricing and
utilization.
Risks to Upside
n Pricing power is sustained in 2026.
n Listing on main board attracts investment fund flows.

n Aluminum process enjoys higher margins.
Risks to Downside
n Competition from China intensifies.
n Price erosion is faster than expected.
n Demand from consumer electronics clients weakens further.
n Inventory in the supply chain takes longer to digest.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

嘉澤：全球前三大 CPU Socket 供應商，伺服器已成營收主軸
全球前三大中央處理器插座CPU socket供應商。依Bishop統計在電腦暨週邊連
接器廠商中全球排名第四，不分行業別的全球連接器排名全球排名第十八。
主要客戶為AMD與Intel。產品涵蓋 CPU Socket、記憶體插槽、高速連接器及
線材，並延伸至 SOCAMM、液冷快接頭與 NPO Socket。
本研究預估，嘉澤伺服器營收由 2026 年221.14 億元增至 2027 年 328.67 億元，
年增 48.6%。成長來自板量回升、CPU新平台提高 Socket 與高速連接器價值，
以及 AI 新產品放量；PC 相關營收占比則由 2025 年 36.60%，降至 2027 年
16.5%，營運重心持續轉向伺服器。

代理 AI 讓 CPU 工作量增加：從回覆問題，走向執行任務
原先：用戶提出問題；應用程式向模型發送提示；模型生成答案；應用程式將答
案傳回給用戶。主節點CPU負責調度、I/O和系統管理，而GPU則負責繁重的運
算任務。
現在：代理AI不再只是回答一個問題，而是將目標分解成多個步驟，決定下一步
該做什麼，調用多個模型，查詢資料庫，連接API，運行企業應用程序，檢查權
限，檢索內存，驗證輸出，然後再次循環執行整個過程。
CPU負責：編排：管理分解複雜任務的引擎。代理執行和工具呼叫：觸發 API 和
傳統企業軟體。策略與安全：對每個自主操作進行實際檢查。
隨 AI 從模型推論延伸至 Agent 執行，CPU 的角色擴及任務協調、工具操作及資
料處理。AMD 5 月官方文章已描述 CPU：GPU 由過去約 1：4–8，朝 1：1 移
動；Intel Q2 法說進一步表示，目前以顆數計已接近均衡。兩家原廠的觀察，支
持 CPU 在 AI 基礎建設中重要性提高

資料來源 :AMD官網(智能體人工智慧改變了CPU/GPU的平衡)
5

AI 基礎設施正在從「以 GPU 為中心」的架構，走向「GPU 與 CPU 分工協
作」的分散式架構。未來不是只增加 GPU，而是新增獨立的 Agentic CPU
compute layer
New Agentic CPU Racks：新增的 Agent 執行層負責 Agent Control
Plane 與 Tool Execution管理 agent 的任務拆解、工作流調度、狀態管理與
多 agent 協作執行 API 呼叫、SQL 查詢、文件檢索、資料處理、程式執行與
外部工具調用處理權限控管、稽核紀錄、 安全檢查、重試與人工審批核心功
能是把 LLM 的「決策」轉換成企業系統中的「實際行動」

Meta Muse：個人代理產品化，使 CPU 執行環境的需求更具體
Muse 的投資意義在於：大型消費平台開始提供可持續執行任務的個人代理，使
CPU 沙盒需求從技術架構走向實際服務。
與AMD的關係Meta已部署數百萬顆AMD EPYC，並是Venice、Verano 的領先
客戶。因此，Meta 的運算擴張是 AMD CPU 需求的重要觀察對象。
持續在線的虛擬機（Persistent VMs）： Meta Muse 的底層採用了 Muse
Secure VM 架構。這些 VM 即使在使用者沒有主動點開 App 時，也在後台持續
運作，消耗大量的作業系統配置、記憶體管理與安全驗證資源—這些全部屬於通
用運算，只有 CPU 能做。因此當億萬名用戶的 AI 代理同時在線，CPU 的短缺
會遠比 GPU 來得更強烈。Muse 這樣的個人代理規模不斷擴大，對 CPU 的需求
勢必會增長，預計 CPU 與 GPU 的比例將從 1:1往4:1以上走 (intel電話會議)

資料來源 :trendforce, meta
對運算需求的意義Muse 是甚麼
大型平台投入代理服務產品
化
2026 年 9 月 8 日推出個人
AI 代理
執行環境需要 CPU、記憶體
及系統資源
使用專屬安全虛擬機 Muse
Secure VM
工具操作與程式執行增加具備瀏覽器，可跨應用處理工作
工作量不完全取決於即時聊

 Meta Muse 在 9 月 8 日剛上線時，市場仍在觀望它與傳統的問答機器人（如 ChatGPT）

顯示 Muse 已經連續數天登頂美國 App Store 免費榜冠軍，13 天內累計下載突破 250 萬
次。這時市場才驚覺，這是一個「現象級爆款」。
 Muse 可以實際幫用戶串接 Gmail、自動比價，甚至與 Shopify 的 Shop Pay 整合代客結
帳。當週一（9 月 21 日）開盤，市場資金首先推升 Meta 股價大漲 11%。CPU類股全面
大漲10%，嘉澤隔日也開盤即漲停，顯示市場有定位上的連動。(如下圖)
 截至9/30，目前該應用僅在美國與加拿大上線2 2天突破了 500 萬次下載。相比之下，
ChatGPT 達到 500 萬次下載花了 56 天，Grok 花了 103 天，Claude 則耗時 492 天。市

場發現 AI 正從「回答問題」走向「替人類實際操作」的實際應用，引發了 AI Agent的邁
向普及的轉折點。

開發NPO socket，為未來潛在商機
資料來源 :moneyDJ、利成光電科技有限公司
圖片來源：OIF 的《Co-Packaging Framework》第 10 頁、圖 2(d) 21
NPO的核心定義是：將光引擎從傳統的前面板可插拔位置，移動到更靠近交
換晶片（ASIC） 的地方，但光引擎和ASIC仍然是兩個獨立的封裝體。它們通
常被並排安裝在同一塊高性能基板（如矽中介層或有機基板）上
NPO Socket已進入客戶最後認證階段，且達可量產狀態，量產目標落在2027
年第3季，目前未納入估計，等待未來公司給更多指引。
AI資料中心在Scale-up與Scale-out兩種架構同步拉高頻寬需求，嘉澤在NPO、
既有高速銅傳輸與高功率連接方案的產品組合，將對應不同平台導入時程。

時碩 (4566  TT)  Trading Buy

2H26 營運明顯增長 ，投資建議 Trading Buy 。  
投 資 建 議 Trading Buy ： 雖 目 前 時碩 (4566 TT) 處於 CB 緘 默 期 ，
未 提 供 明 確 展 望 ， 但 受 惠 於 航 太 、 散 熱 產 品 出 貨 增 長 ，3Q26 營
運 已 明 顯 攀 升 。 考 量 2026 、 2027 年 營 運 動 能 向 上 ， 投 資 建 議
Trading Buy ， 目 標 價 92 元 (以 202 7 年 EPS 及 PE  17 倍 評 價 )。  
時 碩 積 極 布 局 高 成 長 潛 力 的 利 基 型 市 場 ， 包 含 液 冷 散 熱 系 統 、
人 形 機 器 人 零 組 件 ： 液冷散熱 方面， 時 碩 致 力 於 提 供 一 站 式 的
全 方 位 熱 管 理 解 決 方 案 ， 其 產 品 線 涵 蓋 快 接 頭 、 分 歧 管 、 冷 卻
液 分 配 單 元 、 微 通 道 板 及 技 術 門 檻 較 高 的 水 冷 板 ， 並 能 提 供 不
鏽 鋼 與 銅 等 多 樣 材 質 。公司 已 成 功 獲 得 美 、 日 系 液 冷 系 統 大 廠
訂單，且於 2H26 放量 出貨 ， 預估 散 熱 產 品 佔 202 6 年 營 收 比 重
有望 接近 10% 。 人 形 機 器 人方 面 ， 時 碩 積 極 拓 展 此 領 域 的 關 鍵
零 組 件 業 務 ， 產 品 涵 蓋 諧 波 減 速 機 關 鍵 零 組 件 、 機 器 手 臂 關 節
精 密 機 械 加 工 零 件 。 時 碩 目 前 已 打 入 美 國 與 中 國 廠 商 的 供 應 鏈
體 系 ； 中 國 無 錫 廠 藉 由 地 緣 優 勢 積 極 切 入 關 節 零 件 製 造 。 泰 國
春 武 里 新 廠也 規 劃 將 產 線 逐 步 延 伸 至 機 器 人 相 關 應 用 領 域 。 
3Q26 營 收 明 顯 躍 升，預估 2026 、2027 年 營 運 動 能 向 上：雖 1H26
獲 利 表 現 平 平 ， 但 受 惠 於 航 太 、 散 熱 應 用 出 貨 明 顯 增 長 帶 動 ，
3Q26 營收 18.18 億元， QoQ+23.8 6%、 YoY+43.23% ； 且 出 貨 動
能 有 望 延 伸 至 4Q26 。2026 年 方 面 ，汽 車 、 航 太 、 工 業 三 大 領 域
皆有望較 2025 年 增 長。汽 車 應 用 方 面， 雖 中 國 市 場 競 爭 仍 然 激
烈，但 因 切 入 新 能 源 車 領 域 且 客 戶 需 求 帶 動 下，營 收穩 定 增 長。
航太領域 方面， 天 陽 航 太 已 通 過 多 項 航 太 認 證 ， 成 功 打 入 全 球
前 兩 大 飛 機 製 造 商 及 Tier 1 供 應 商 ， 預 估 營 收 將維 持 高 速 成 長
態勢 。 工 業 應 用 方 面 ， 既 有 領 域 有 望 較2025 年 改 善 ， 且 新 成 長
動 能 將 來 自 於 液 冷 散 熱 相 關 產 品 及 人 形 機 器 人 零 組 件 。 預 估
2026 年 營 收 66.00 億 元 ，YoY+ 32.62 %， 毛 利 率 維 持 高 檔 ， 營 業
利益 YoY+ 97.15 %。 預 估 稅 後 純 益 3.71 億 元 ， YoY+ 148.83 %，
EPS 3.36 元。 隨 著 航 太 、 散 熱 應 用 持 續 發 酵 ， 且 泰 國 新 廠 建 置
完 成 ， 預 估 2027 年 營 收 、 獲 利 動 能 向 上 。

  時碩 (4566  TT) E SG ：  
時碩的 ESG 政 策 由 「 永 續 發 展 暨 風 險 管 理 委 員 會 」 統 籌 ， 並 由 「 風 險 管 理
推 動 委 員 會 」 負 責 執 行 與 追 蹤，將 氣 候 變 遷 及 各 項 永 續 議 題 納 入 公 司 整 體 策
略 與 風 險 管 理 流 程 中。在 環 境 面，公 司 依 循 國 際 標 準 進 行 溫 室 氣 體 盤 查 與 第
三 方 確 信 ， 並 透 過 節 能 設 備 導 入 、 能 源 管 理 與 監 測 系 統 、 製 程 優 化 、 太 陽 能
及綠電等多元措施推動減碳與能源效率提升，同時強化水資源與廢棄物管
理 ， 藉 由 回 收 再 利 用 、 廢 水 循 環 及 資 源 化 設 備 ， 落 實 減 量 及 循 環 經 濟 ； 原 物
料 與 包 材 則 遵 循 RoHS 、REACH 等 規 範，降 低 製 程 對 環 境 的 衝 擊。社 會 面 方
面，時 碩 重 視 員 工 薪 資 福 利、培 訓 與 職 涯 發 展，推 動 性 別 平 等 與 管 理 階 層 多
元 化，並 建 立 職 業 安 全 衛 生 制 度 及 教 育 訓 練，以 降 低 職 災 風 險；在 供 應 鏈 則
透 過 定 期 稽 核 與 評 分 機 制，提 升 供 應 商 在 品 質、交 期 與 服 務 上 的 表 現，並 以
產 品 品 質 與 安 全 為 核 心 ， 確 保 生 產 與 產 品 符 合 相 關 要 求 。 同 時 ， 公 司 以「 企
業 公 民 」 自 我 定 位，投 入 社 會 公 益 與 在 地 關 懷，減 少 營 運 對 社 區 的 負 面 影 響，
並 維 持 完 善 的 資 訊 安 全 管 理 以 避 免 資 訊 外 洩。在 治 理 面，時 碩 建 構 包 含 獨 立
董 事 與 女 性 董 事 的 董 事 會 結 構，設 置 薪 酬 與 審 計 等 功 能 性 委 員 會，訂 定 涵 蓋
營 運 、 財 務、 法 遵 、 環 境 與 氣 候 變 遷 、 人 資 與 人 權 等 面 向 的 風 險 管 理 政 策 ，
並 透 過 法 說 會、網 站 與 永 續 報 告 與 利 害 關 係 人溝 通，同 時 積 極 參 與 各 項 ESG
獎 項 評 選 ， 以 強 化 公 司 治 理 與 永 續 形 象 。

### Page 6

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 19 日

6 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15 %

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

2027 年重回成長軌道

永豐觀點 
系統電連三個月營收創下新高，預期在 BBU 及無人載具開始出貨下，營收逐月成

 投資評價與建議

成長 ， 然研究員仍維持中立的投資建議 ， 主係考量(1) 營運槓桿效應須待 2H27 時，
BBU 放量出貨較明顯。此外，系統電為因應未來產能持續擴充 BBU 及無人機產線
侵蝕獲利；(2) 評價上，以 2027 年稀釋後稅後 EPS 2.17 元計算，目前本益比約
30 倍，評價尚屬合理，故維持中立的投資建議，持續關注 BBU 出貨及新訂單狀
況。

### Page 2

2

系統電營運主力將轉移至 BBU 相關應用： 系統電子工業股份有限公司成立於1977
年，原專注在車用電子、能源、通訊、電玩遊戲。近年公司陸續進行業務調整及轉
型 ， 將業務分為汽車電子 、 工業物聯網 、AI 資料中心能源系統管理三大事業 ，2015
年推出藍牙胎壓偵測器(BLE TPMS)， 目前主要經營業務內容為車用胎壓偵測系統 、
車用電子產品、綠色能源及儲能設備、電源供應器，並獲有多項專利，並持續擴大
BLE TPMS 應用在歐美 OE 前裝市場，如新能源訂動車、大客車車隊、皮卡等。近
期受到資料中心用電需求增加，系統電 BBU 相關產品將開始出貨。1H26 產品比
重：TPMS 46%、工業電腦 38%、AI 資料中心能源系統及其他 16%。 
3Q26 強固型工業電腦出貨旺，：系統電 9 月營收 3.67 億元(+12.04%MoM，
+17.87%YoY)，連續三個月創下歷史新高，主係因強固型電腦產品出貨。雖 BBU
及無人機相關產品尚未大量出貨，然在工業電腦領域，受惠產業趨勢帶動，如農耕
車及無人載具相關應用成長快速，使系統電 3Q26 營收 10.14 億元(+13%QoQ，
+24%YoY)，研究處預估毛利率 23.2%(+1ppt QoQ，+1.9ppt YoY)，因積極投入
研發及新產品設計使營業費用較高，稅後淨利 0.04 億元，稅後 EPS 為 0.02 元。 
4Q26 兩大專案 BBU 及無人機小量出貨 ：先前耕於已久的 BBU 產品將於十月開始
出貨 ， 初期以3kW 產品為主 ， 系統電已完成建置 2 條全自動 與 1 條半自動 BBU
產線。無人機方面，包含軍用及微型軍警偵察需求強勁，產線亦完成建置。研究處
預期 4Q26 營收 11.17 億元(+10.2%QoQ，+28%YoY)，毛利率 24%(+0.8ppt 
QoQ，-0.6ppt YoY)，稅後淨利 0.07 億元，稅後 EPS 為 0.03 元。預估 2026 年營
收 38.9 億元(+14.7%YoY)，毛利率 23.6%(+0.1pts YoY) ，稅後淨利 0.24 億元(-
54.3%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 0.11 元。 
2027 年迎來營運收成期：展望 2027 年，以三大事業體區分，工業電腦方面將聚
焦於邊緣算力模組、GCS 地面控制站，部門產品毛利率最佳，有利於獲利表現；AI
資料中心能源系統方面，BBU 產品將開始大量出貨，並持續擴充產能，預估部門
營收將年增 328%，並將增加 3-4 條 BBU 產線，因應未來新產品的加入；TPMS
業務收到車市影響，營運相對平淡。綜上所述，系統電2027 年營運將開始收成過
去在無人載具及 BBU 電源產品的投入，研究處預期 2027 年營收 61.68 億元
(+58.5%YoY)，毛利率 24.9%(+1.3pts YoY) ，稅後淨利 5.41 億元，稅後 EPS 為
2.36 元。

## Document 247: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports. 1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.
Exhibit 1: FDI Inflows to the U.S.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

4 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.
Exhibit 3: Europe's Upcoming Elections Represent Around Half of EU GDP and Population.

European 2026/2027 Cycle

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026, 2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

## Document 280: Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14)

14 October 2026
The bank
for a changing

EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.
SOURCE: S&P GLOBAL, BNP PARIBAS

These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

### Page 1

Equity Research 
Sector Report | Technology｜Oct 15, 2026 
Oct Edition: Choppy Markets, Intact AI

Overseas  
Technology

Jeff Pu, CFA 
SFC CE No. BNO719 
jeffpu@gfgroup.com.hk 
Henry Huang 
SFC CE No. BYA749 
henryhuang@gfgroup.com.hk

Market to remain choppy: Tech equities were volatile in the past month, with rotation 
toward hyperscaler and internet names. That said , s emi and memory (Korea) 
weakness reflected lower risk appetite due to rising bond yields, AI monetization/ARR 
visibility and L-T AI debates (i.e., Broadcom CEO warned that capital may now be a 
bigger constraint), even as company-specific catalysts continued to be strong. On the 
other hand, the positive was the acceleration of AI model scaling (i.e., Meta’s Muse 
Wonder, Gemini 4, etc). That said, w e view the pullback primarily as a reset in 
expectations rather than the evidence of AI deterioration. Despite light positioning, we 
expect the markets will remain choppy in the near term but would  accumulate low 
valuation & sustainable names  (i.e., HBM, non-TSMC foundries, CPUs, CPOs, etc) . 
In our preferred list, we added CLS, while removing MTK and Hon Hai.  
U.S. roadshow takeaways (week of Oct 5): Investor tone skewed neutral to slightly 
cautious, driven by conservative top ‑down macro views, U.S. election, and ongoing 
debates around the L-T AI demand. Positioning in AI infrastructure has come off peak 
levels. Even  so, many investors would accumulate on pullbacks, with interest 
concentrated in “Made in U.S.” names (INTC, LITE, COHR), U.S. memory suppliers, 
TSMC, and selective bottleneck themes — including ASE, ABF substrates, 
MRVL/SMTC, and Japanese MLCCs. 
OCP & earnings season in focus: The week is anchored by AI/semis — SEMICON 
West, OCP, and earnings from ASML, TSMC, etc. OCP reinforced the industry’s pivot 
toward rack‑scale infrastructure, with inference and agentic workloads driving demand 
across compute, memory, and networking. Scale‑up optics, NPO/CPO, and 800VDC 
power were key themes, while AMD’s CPU outlook and Helios/Helion underscored a 
secular CPU uptrend. Nvidia ’s use of PTFE for NVLink boards further supports our 
positive stance on PTFE (see PCB Spec in 2 H27, Oct 5).  On earnings, ASML 
delivered largely in -line results, GM and OP above consensus with revenue slightly 
miss, and raised full year guidance again and is exploring ways to increase capacity 
by >30% 2028 to fulfill strong customer demand, also extending the order visibility 
beyond 2028. For TSMC, we anticipate an inline quarter. 
September monthly sales/data: TSMC sales -0.6% MoM, slightly ahead. Rubin to 
accelerate and T3 ramp. PC forecast trimmed. Substrate/CCL upcycle with 
pricing/margin expansion. Aspeed revenue +101% YoY. Details on page 5.

Sector Report | Technology

GFHK Tech Important Stock Calls  
In the past one month, our key reports and stock calls are: 
Jeff Pu on ASIC Updates on TPU v9, reported date on Oct 13: We expect near-term MTK headwinds 
from TPU v9 delays but remain constructive on MTK/MRVL longer term. Humufish’s redesign could 
push tape-out to Jan -27, MP tape -out to 2Q27 and shipments to 1Q28, while EMIB -T yields could 
recover from 45–50%. Rising TPU allocations to Anthropic could constrain Gemini compute, though 
higher internal allocation in 2H26 may support future models. SemiAnalysis expects Anthropic/OpenAI 
to each add 5 –10GW in 2027 vs. a few GW for Google. For TPU v10 (4Q28 /1Q29), MTK/GUC’s 
Icefish remains the only confirmed mainstream SKU, while Broadcom-led Merope faces AMD/Marvell 
competition, with both potentially testing EMIB-T. 
Michelle Jing on PCB Spec in 2H27, reported date on Oct 5: Some market watchers expect Nvidia 
to shift from PTFE to hydrocarbon resin (HC) for the VR300 NVL576 switch tray. HC—one of the glass 
free alternatives within PTFE hybrid architectures—has only recently passed SI testing and has yet to 
enter PCB MP, leaving it roughly three months behind PTFE in qualification. On the contrary, we 
believe that PTFE offers superior performance for high frequency, high speed designs. With VR300 
NVL576 shipments expected in 2H27 and the final configuration likely set by end 26 —and with a 
leading Chinese PCB ma ker already achieving meaningful PTFE hybrid yields —we maintain that 
PTFE hybrid remains the most probable solution for next year’s switch board. 
Alicia Xia on CPO Acceleration , reported date on  Sept 29 : Nvidia’s CPO scale -out switch is 
progressing faster than expected, with FAU yields improving. We forecast CPO switch shipments at 
15k/16k/30k in 4Q26/1Q27/2Q27 and 100k in 2027. For Nvidia/AWS scale -up, CPO and NPO 
continue in parallel, with full NPO adoption expected at NVL576 launch. Feynman is also devel oping 
both variants, with CPO potentially using QM6 + six 6.4T OEs. We shift AWS Trainium 4 launch to 
1Q28 and forecast global scale-up OE demand at 6.5m/42m in 2027E/28E. 
Jeff Pu on NAND Re -acceleration, reported date on Sept 20 : According to TrendForce, NAND 
demand is stronger than expected as U.S. CSPs scale AI infrastructure, with eSSD demand nearly 
2x prior forecasts. We raise NAND contract price growth to +21%/+17%/+15% QoQ in 
3Q26/4Q26/1Q27, while hyperscaler inventories decline. Supply remains disci plined at 15%/21%/20% 
YoY bit growth in 2025 –27. Demand is supported by agentic AI, vector databases and KV -cache 
offloading, where eSSD offers lower cost/bit than DRAM and better power efficiency than HDD. 
Jeff Pu on TSMC 3Q26 Preview , reported date on  Oct 7 : TSMC shares hit record highs on 
expectations ahead of the print and potential Terafab collaboration. We expect largely inline 3Q26 
results, capex and full-year guidance, with softer 4Q26 margin (67.2%). Management should reaffirm 
2026 capex at the ceiling and mid-40s YoY growth, while we forecast 37% growth in 2027 on sustained 
AI/CPU/networking demand and price hikes. Key focuses include margins, long -term capex/U.S. 
expansion, large-reticle packaging and A14. We raise 2026E/27E EPS by 2%/8% on hig her N2/N3 
capacity and operating leverage, and roll to 20x 2027E P/E, lifting TP to NT$2,960.

### Page 5

Sector Report | Technology

September Monthly Data  
TSMC September sales of NT$511.9bn, -0.6% MoM and +54.6% YoY, with total 3Q revenue reaching 
NT$1.49T, +17.6 QoQ, slightly ahead of expectations of +14%. The solid performance was well 
supported by ASIC, A20 Pro, and Rubin. For 3Q26, we expect gross margin to be 67.8%, higher than 
guidance of 65-67% and consensus of 66.4% . For 4Q26, we expect another 12% QoQ growth with 
GM at 67.2%, both ahead of street’s estimates of +10% QoQ and 66% margin. Ahead of the earnings 
call, key focus include gross margin trajectory, long -term capex discipline, and the execution of its 
U.S. fab expansion. 
Rubin to accelerate:  Hon Hai revenue last month was NT$1.16T, +26 % MoM, +38% YoY, mainly 
driven by iPhone build.  Quanta and Wistron delivered -23% and -17% MoM respectively, due to AI 
racks’ shipping timing differences. Of note, Pegatron surprised the market with +143.2% MoM revenue 
surge fueled by initial GB300 rack shipments to neocloud customers including Lambda and Together 
AI. This reaffirms our thesis of robust NVL72 order momentum across Neoclouds. Looking ahead, 
VR200 is ramping now, with volume deployment accelerating into 2027. We maintain our forecast of 
75k and 92k NVL72 (GB+VR) rack shipments for 2026 and 2027, respectively. Concurrently, Wiwynn 
delivered another solid month with revenue expanding 20.0% MoM, underpinne d by resilient GP 
server demand alongside the ramp of Trainium 3. 
ASIC ramping up: On design service vendors fronts, driven  by T3 ramp, Alchip’s Sep revenue 
reached a record NT$9.9 8bn. Backed by secured incremental 3nm wafer allocation and improving 
yields, management raised its 2027 revenue guidance, now targeting growth above the previous 50% 
YoY baseline. That said, we push out T4 shipment timeline into 1Q28. GUC delivered a strong beat 
with monthly revenue reaching a record NT$7.145bn (+22.4% MoM, +97.8 % YoY), driven by robust 
Axion 2 CPU demand. Beyond its engagement with top 4 CSPs, multi -year order visibility into 2027 
and beyond is further bolstered by high-margin (likely TK1) automotive ASIC designs. 
PC forecast trimmed: NB shipments from top 5 ODMs were 9.7m in Sep, with 3Q26 shipments down 
17% QoQ and 26% YoY. Global PC OEM shipments contracted 20.1% YoY in 3Q26, worse than 
expected, post robust 1H26 pull -ins with high pricing points and weak macro. For 2027, we project 
the industry will decline a further 5% YoY. On the competitive front, tier -1 vendors such as Lenovo, 
Dell, and HP are not immune to this downturn, while entry-level laptops (low cost models from China) 
continue to see stronger relative demand.  
Substrate/CCL Upcycle Intact: Unimicron continued to deliver another all-time high sales with +5% 
MoM and 65% YoY. Supported by ABF upward pricing of ~15% in 2H26 (likely more next year) and 
higher mix of AI products, we expect a meaningful GM uptick for this quarter with momentum to sustain 
into 2027 . In the meantime, ZengDing’s revenue rose 28% MoM and 36% YoY mainly driven by  
iPhone, 1.6T mSAP, and Rubin’s HDI. On the CCL front, bolstered by ongoing price hikes and strong 
demand from GP servers, ITEC revenue grew 15.5% MoM and 111% YoY, and NanYa Plastics 
recorded +12.5% MoM and +55% YoY. Meanwhile, EMC and TUC both posted another single digit 
growth MoM on sales.

Downstream tech – Smartphone & Apple  
We maintain Global smartphone forecast of -14% in 2026  
According to IDC, the preliminary 2Q26 smartphone data points to a still ‑soft market with uneven 
regional dynamics, and their commentary continues to emphasize structural rather than cyclical 
headwinds. Smartphone shipments remain under pressure from weak replacement cycles, cautious 
consumer spending, and limited breakthrough features. IDC typically highlights that “growth is 
concentrated in the premium tier,” driven by ecosystem lock ‑in, while the mass market remains 
challenged by price sensitivity. They also tend to note that China and parts of emerging markets show 
only modest stabilization, not a full recovery, and that vendors are prioritizing margin discipline over 
volume.  
Overall, by factoring in higher iPhone 17/17 Pro volume, we fine tune our global smartphone shipment 
forecast to -14% YoY from -15% YoY. 
For 2027, we expect global smartphone shipments to be flattish YoY. That said, 2026 -2027 will be 
one of the most challenging periods for the industry, driven primarily by sharply higher memory prices, 
which have already pushed OEMs to reduce shipments and raise device prices.
